--- Start of Factions notes.txt ---
Tier list:

S: Kestrel, PanO, Yujin

A: Haqquislam, Neoterran, Morat, Tak, MO, Bakunin, O12

B: Combined, Steel Phalanx, Tunguska, Qapu Khalqi, Varuna, Starmada, Invincible Army, OSS,  Shock, White Company, Ariadna, Hassassin, White Banner, Next Wave, Caledonian

-------

PanO Vanilla
---
Direct gunfight (best gunfighter)
high-tech
Good TAGs
Suffer vision control/objective play (WIP not good)

PanO - Svalarheima Winter Force
---
Simple/direct gameplan
Some good CC
Can ignore hacking (can run without heavy infantry)
Easy to collect (1 action box mostly)
Powerful TAGs
Can't find minis

PanO - Kestrel
---
Not as straightforward, hacking is a must
TAGs are smaller and lighter
Can do everything


Yu Jing
---
Jack-of-all-trades
Hvy infantry and big guns
Adaptable on table and list building
Few apex units

White Banner
---
Simple tools/fireteams
Easy to collect
Indirect defense is less punishing

Imperial Secret Service (ISS)
---
Deva + Dakini
Zhanying duo, Adil Mehmit, Koga Ninja (minelayer)
Complicated interaction
Opaque list construction

Invincible Army
---
Challenging to pilot
Can be all heavy infantry

Ariadna

Aggressive + relies on hidden info, low tech, werewolf
Light skirmisher focus
Can ignore hacking
Fragile
Somewhat recommended...

Tartary Army Corps (TAK)
---
Strong direct combat potential + camo/hidden
Lean unit with clear roles
Easy to collect
Strong ablative defense

Kosmoflot
---
Slightly inferior gunfight
Can rely on some key units that are hard to use

USAriadna
---
May be on the way out
Lacks lots of tools

Haqqislam
---
Unique faction - uses asymmetric warfare
Infiltrating specialist and scalpel units
Strong hacking and doctors
Very fragile
Biomedically augmented soldiers
Lots of irregular troops

Ramah Task Force
---
Variety of tools and units, easy understood
Flexible fireteams and list building
Positive face to face engagement with minimal setup

Hassassin Bahram
---
Complex and punishing entry point to game
Feels like playing a different game
Lots of assassins

Nomads
--
hacking focused
excellent vision control
Unique individual units
Lack redundancy and run out of resources

Bakunin 
---
Durable and forgiving units
Inuitive and powerful fireteams
Strong unique units
Easy to collect (Reverend Moira action pack)
Lots of variety

Tunguska
---
Some key units are hard to collect

Corregidor
---
Frustrating to build and play

Combined Army (aliens)
---
Top-heavy power faction
Specialized skills and equipment unique to faction (lieut skills, ammo types, equip.)
Strong units in every category
High durability and snowball potential

Morat
---
Excel in direct combat, but still standard tools/tech
Don't have to worry about command structure or isolation
Easy to collect (action pack)

Onyx Contact Force
---
Complex entry point
Harder to collect

Shesvasty
---
not great

Aleph (AI/Robots/artificial humans)
---
High tech, excel in infowar and ranged
Strong chars, apex units, unique skills
Fun interplay and synergy between units
Fantastic durable specialists

Steel Phalanx
---
Hard to hit/forgiving units
Strong order generation and line troops
Very easy to collect

OSS
---
Good be not as great as steel phalanx
Hacking and robot support troops

O-12
---
High-tech, can bring the entire toolbox in a list
Purpose-made model for every role
Minor focus on non-lethal weapons (strong)
Superior command structure

Torchlight Brigade
---
Durable heavy infantry faction with tools to cover their weakness
Hard-to-punish command structure
Strong variety of defense options (Moonraker)
Unique power pieces (Wrecker, Hippolada)

Starmada
---
Also good
Harder to collect
Non-lethal is less intuitive

JSA

Mix of infantry brawling, rapid engagement, specialized hidden units
Cheap hvy inf. and cost-effective light inf.
Great CC and getting to CC
Challenging to play/unique

Shindenbutai
---
Fun and varied faction
Advanced tech (plasma wpn, disco ball, pitchers, etc)

Oban
---
Funky army more of a challenge to start



--- End of Factions notes.txt ---

--- Start of Hassassin Bahram.txt ---
Hassassin Bahram
---
Ghulam: line, good utility pieces, FT, smoke gren launch, 1-2 min
Hassassin Barid: pitcher, must: 1 hacker, 1 killer hacker (FT)
Asawira: HI, 2 (FT)
Hassassin Muyibs: good/optional, tac aware, FT duo
Hassassin Lasiqs: great shooters, FT duo
Hassassin Govads: MSV HMG/AP Sniper
Husam Op Leila Sharif: anti-stealth, duo with Garam gunfighter
Ghazi Muttawiah: irreg, smoke, with baggage (shoot jammers)
Hassassin Fiday: solo, great infil, mines great, also smoke
McMurrough: ok, smoke, CC
Hassassin Nadhir: ambusher, ok only
Hassassin Shuaje: ambusher, better, minelayer 2
Hassassin Ayyar: ambusher, HI, great
Hassassin Farzan: ambusher, great, minelayer
Daylami: irr, solid
Sunduqbut: HI, too expensive
Hassassin Ragik: good jump, for mission or hacking
Shakush: ok TAG
Kamel: baggage

--- End of Hassassin Bahram.txt ---

all of your defenses, then the close assault can move in and really start to dismantle you, and at that point, you've

these other factors in keeping your head up, using smart air rolls, holding the

hidden elements and have a good understanding of what they're trying to do. You want to defensively pin units

want to bring are ranged weapons over 32 in like sniper rifles or missile launchers, units who can rapidly engage
in the midfield once you've been able to lure those units and support structures out and network projection or EM weapons
to really make light of the fact of just how hackable all of the main strike pieces in Kestrel are. Alternative tools
are very strong close assault who can move in if you don't have network projection or EM weapons. A strong
midfield presence to zone or push them out of that midfield can be very
frustrating. And in a blade of defense to stop that sort of defensive endgame
of them dismantling all of your I guess defensive equity.

--- Start of JSA unit analysis.txt ---
JSA -  Shindenbutai

Metsuke Officer: great lieutenant option, counterintel, holoproj for defense,
strategos L1 is great
lieut +1 if you have NCO (Hatamoto)
Kestrel Magistrate as Proxy
Senku: bad, cheap
can link to hatamotto, or
cheap out on lieutenant,
could skip competely
Tanuki: great, esp for a haris
hacker: best concentration of utility in game, can be duo with rui shi, disco baller
hvy machine gun with MSV2 is great but expensive
chain of command can be good
Fennec as proxies
Yaoxie Rui Shi:
remote, really good with Tanuki
Can run all Yu Jing, all JSA
Any drone as proxy
Yamabushi:
FTO for fire team, but need one Tanuki
Camouflage prob better
Paramedic FTO might be good in a bushitai FT
Can run in Kestrel (!)
Shizoku: shooting beast, great brawler, in Haris is great (Tanuki, Rui Shi, Shizoku)
Jizamurai: ok, necessary to create a bushitai fireteam (ex. with Shizoku, Yamabushi paramedic)
Can move 6 + dodge 4 to move
+ E/Mitter or Thunderbolt is worth it
Hatamoto: can run single, or with Jizamurai, expensive
fast assault piece, can move straight line,
sole NCO (for lieut +1)
nanoscreen doesn't interact with Frenzy
special ops
Kyojin Killers: good CC, affordable
irregular, hvy flamethrower + flammenspeer
Fennec or scarecrow as proxy
Tenshin: main killer hacker, needed
likely deploy normally to use as killer hacker
mobile specialist
Ninjas: ok killer hacker,
can run in Yu Jing, Oban
Kurayami ninjas: ok, but no camouflage
Yaoxie Lu Duan: high utility piece, shoot once, surprise atk, holo proj come back
thunderbolt is good with BS +1B
Can run all Yu Jing, all JSA
Drones as proxy
Monstruckers: build defensive net by itself, great with combat remotes
irregular, but can always spend its order (stuff to do if you have baggage)
engineer, AP turret, mines, drop bears
can run in Ramah, O-12 and others
JSA/PanO Eng guy as proxy
Yojimbo: irreg, good for smoke grenades, esp. with MSV2 units
Also Oban
Kyujin as proxy
Yaopu Pangguling:
EVO hacking
Can run all Yu Jing, all JSA
Drone as proxy
Aibot Uran missile launcher
Also Oban
Drone as proxy
Aibot Garon smoke launcher
Also Oban
Drone as proxy
Warcors: can run all teams
Fennec as proxy
--- End of JSA unit analysis.txt ---

If you're coming to this video after 1st April 2025, be aware that it's an April Fools video - however, it's also 50 minutes of me playing a mostly straight bat in terms of actual discussion about Kestrel to set up a joke in the last thirty seconds. As such, the video remains quite watchable - just don't, whatever you do, take the actual rankings given to any unit seriously. Unless you have a bad case of PanO brain, in which case never mind and it will all make total sense.
so basically everything in the video was fairly serious except the rankings. However, any commentary about how good minelayer turrets is obviously needs to be read as outdated now.

--- Start of kfc unit analysis.txt ---
PanOceania - Kestrel Colonial Force

Fennec: great line troops,
maybe could use as Lieutenant,
likely active attacks done by others in Fireteam
thunderbolt + shock mine is awesome
hacker + E/Mitter, Fast panda, is awesome (even more than one)
Fwd observer, sensor + disco baller, is awesome
Paramedic (or use Pandra) + medkit
BCA Magistrate
primary choice for lieutenant (top 5 lieut in game)
counterintelligence
strategos L1. combi rifle, nano pulser
Can link with Jackals to convert irregular order to reg
Blink Team: fantastic, as standalone unit
Hvy rocket, with XVisor, wild parrot and assault pistol
hvy rocket ignore cover, so similar to +3 BS
ignore other profiles
Drummers + Drumbot: maybe good? Maybe super vulnerable for price/glass cannon
can be buff with evo hacker if 2 drummers
Drumbot_1 multi hvy machine gun is best fighter
Drumbot_2 if enough SWC
Drumbot_5 good objective runners, since they both roll for objective, and cheap
Won't lose the order if bot dies, and is main fighter
Not super survivable for price
JSA Tech guy + wildparrot as proxy ?
Trauma docs: don't link with anything, not worth it
Machinist: don't link with anything, not much value, better engineer in faction
Kinda have to heal TAG (and AP turrets)
Also some use because of deactivator and D-charges
Palbots are also a significant bonus here allowing you to increase the board presence of your AVA1 machinist
Winter
Tech-Bees: deactivator (good to activate discoballer), Gizmokit, spec. op., TAGCom: buff TAGs, super cheap, good piece
All PanO
Ok for TAG

Good instead of Warcor if you have points

JSA/PanO Medic as proxy
Indigo Richard Quinn: OK, normal Fennec probably better for quality/price
Dr Priya Harper: FTO, tac aware, +1 SWC, expensive but good for the SWC
For some missions

killer hacker great (cybermask works while super-jump)

hacker good too

can duo

Good in Fennec Fireteam

Hetkari: fantastic statline: enemy has -3 BS, Hetkari ignore cover, expensive but worth it
Maybe best gunfighter in the game
Multi red fury is good/safer pick
Lieutenant could be used as solo piece, decent if paired with chain-of-command
Griffin: super good
Fennec keyword (ex. 4 fennec and a Griffin), also kestrel keyword
Hvy machine gun, nice active turn wpn

Perfectly serviceable
All PanO
Drones as proxy
Sierra dronbots: thunderbolt, OK, ARO with Total Reaction, 3 Str + engineer makes it last
Clipper dronbot: not linkable, lame
Fugazi dronbot: good as repeaters, flash pulse ok, cheap, with two + hacker your deployment zone can be covered for hacking

Might not want if not enough hacker on your list (3 needed)
All PanO
Drones as proxy
Mulebot: baggage, useful for panzerfaust, turret, discoballer
Evo hacker can be good. esp. with baggage
All PanO
Drones as proxy
Scarecrow: really good, cheap for hidden deployment
Killer hacker esp with surprise attack through repeater
Fwd obs is good too
Dart: not great
Beasthunter free guild: irregular solid, fantastic
Can run Winter, Yu jing, White Banner, Ramah, and others
Kyojin or Senku as proxy
Yamabushi: regular, camo profile, panzerfaust is good
Can run Shindenbutai
Redeye Close Air Support Vehicule: weapon choices not great, better SWC spend elsewhere
Warcor: good for 3 pts
areocam will help defend your deployment zone being a 360% visor

KCF wants to:

Outgun with rock-paper-scissors style
Use CC or hidden hacker vs hacking
Move in midfield with resilient guns
Create defensive foothold
Use deployable to reduce maneuverability
High-quality doctors/eng to counter-balance lack of redundancy
Take objectives as necessary or in late game

KCF are afraid of:

Gunfighter mismatch
Hackers being scalped
Pushed out of midfield
Their vision control killed
Hidden element being read
Running out of time

immune to retreat

Morat BS not great

standard

--- Start of PanOceania - Kestrel Colonial Force.txt ---
PanOceania - Kestrel Colonial Force

Fennec: great line troops, best in game, best utility
hmg, rockets, not good
likely active attacks done by others in Fireteam
thunderbolt + shock mine is awesome
hacker + E/Mitter, Fast panda for network projection, awesome (even more than one),
Fwd observer, sensor + disco baller, is awesome
Paramedic (or use Pandra) + medkit

BCA Magistrate
primary choice for lieutenant (top 5 lieut in game)
counterintelligence
strategos L1. combi rifle, nano pulser
Can link with Jackals to convert irregular order to reg

Blink Team: fantastic, as standalone unit
Hvy rocket, with XVisor, wild parrot and assault pistol
hvy rocket ignore cover, so similar to +3 BS
ignore other profiles
Not auto pick

Drummers + Drumbot: maybe good? Bof. Maybe super vulnerable for price/glass cannon
can be buff with evo hacker if 2 drummers
Drumbot_1 multi hvy machine gun is best fighter
Drumbot_2 if enough SWC
Drumbot_5 good objective runners, since they both roll for objective, and cheap
Won't lose the order if bot dies, and is main fighter
Not super survivable for price

Trauma docs: don't link with anything, not worth it

Machinist: don't link with anything, not much value, better engineer in faction
Kinda have to heal TAG (and AP turrets)
Also some use because of deactivator and D-charges
Palbots are also a significant bonus here allowing you to increase the board presence of your AVA1 machinist

Tech-Bees: deactivator (good to activate discoballer), Gizmokit, spec. op., TAGCom: buff TAGs, super cheap, good piece
Ok for TAG
Even if no TAG, 3rd flash pulse, cheap order, check being deployment zone
Good instead of Warcor if you have points

JSA/PanO Eng guy as proxy

Jackals: irregulars, cheap, good
FTO link with magistrate (2 jackals) a must, at worst, dui
Light rocket launcher good, Booty
Infiltrator with minelayer is OP, best defense in game

Emily Handelman: not great, better chain of command elsewhere, no link

Knauf: not linkable, not great

Bipandra: doctor, medkit +1B, tactical awareness, not super great, bit expensive
Bipandra inside of the link is an okay specialist
Some say best doctor in game
Tac awareness+doctor reroll
Fennec or kestrel FT:
Ex: Fennec, Bip and crux
Griffin+Bip duo is often the only duo needed

Indigo Richard Quinn: OK, normal Fennec probably better for quality/price

Dr Priya Harper: FTO, tac aware, +1 SWC, expensive but good for the SWC
Banshees:
killer hacker great (cybermask works while super-jump)
Great for objectives becomes of super-jump
hacker good too

Fireflies: great for duo
can duo
Hvy Rocket at 1.5 SWC is great + another utility piece
Chain of command is great, cheap and no SWC
Engineer is nice, esp with dummers or to reactivate discoballers or duo with TAG
Minelayer has armed turret, this one is good
Paramedic is fine
Hacker is decent
Armed turret is now not great, cant be reloaded

Black AIR: fantastic package
All profiles are decent
Good in Fennec Fireteam
Neurocinetics good, could even be solo if Fennec team is full
One of the best ARO piece in an FT, but its is sometimes piece because the Griffin Feuerbach exists

Father Lucien: no link, expensive

Optimate Agent: no great, expensive, crap

Hetkari: fantastic statline: enemy has -3 BS, Hetkari ignore cover, expensive but worth it
Maybe best gunfighter in the game
Multi red fury is good/safer pick
Lieutenant could be used as solo piece, decent if paired with chain-of-command
Good to have speedball or healer
Impure duo with Pathfinder drone prob best FT in game

Griffin: super good, one of the best HI for cost in game
Fennec keyword (ex. 4 fennec and a Griffin), also kestrel keyword
Hvy machine gun, nice active turn wpn
Feuerbach, pulzar, nice well rounded

Crux Father-Knights:  fantastic, but expensive
Good at many things, CC is secondary but you’re not paying a lot for it
Wildcard for linking
FTO, multi marksman, drop bear is well rounded
strat deployment may be worth it
Crux and 2 fennecs is a good team

Tikabalangs + Crabbot: very good
lieut+1 is a must, with chain-of-command (group 2 TAG)
There are good NCOs to use those 4 orders with

Squalos+ Crabbot:
Good, reliable, cheap for quality, can duo

Maximus: Can be good

Pathfinder dronbots: good piece, esp. with TAG, Kestrel team
sensor, tac awareness, wildcard, deactivator for discoball
Perfectly serviceable

Sierra dronbots: thunderbolt, OK, ARO with Total Reaction, 3 Str + engineer makes it last

Clipper dronbot: not linkable, lame

Fugazi dronbot: good as repeaters, flash pulse ok, cheap, with two + hacker your deployment zone can be covered for hacking

Bulleteer armbots: good, repeaters, but not great link options

Peacemaker armbots + auxbots: very good
Peacemaker with aux with minelayer is good
Can put aux bot at 8 inch, then mine at another 8 inch
Repeater good with hacking
Might not want if not enough hacker on your list (3 needed)

Mulebot: baggage, useful for panzerfaust, turret, discoballer
Evo hacker can be good. esp. with baggage

Killer hacker esp with surprise attack through repeater
Fwd obs is good too
Good for infil spec
Jackal a better minelayer

Dart: not great

Beasthunter free guild: irregular solid, fantastic

Yamabushi: regular, camo profile, panzerfaust is good
good deploy zone def

--- End of PanOceania - Kestrel Colonial Force.txt ---

--- Start of PanOceania Impressions.txt ---
Infinity N5: PanOceania – Not-so-Initial Impressions

OK, first thing’s first, introductions are in order: Pan-Oceania is what happens when a near-future USA, Russia, France, UK and Germany get together and agree to collectively bankrupt themselves as the greatest practical joke in history (To note: the real world scoreboard says we’re about 3/5 of the way there already). So, whenever there’s a geo-political power vacuum opening up, of course you’re going to see South America, India, (South, East and South-East) Asia, Australia and for some reason…The Scandinavians*…join forces and become an economic hyperpower. Seems reasonable. Pan-Oceania (PanO for short) is the closest thing to a ‘generic good guys wearing blue’ faction that seems to permeate popular culture. Pro Tip: They aren’t nearly as good as they’d have you believe. PanO, as a faction, are generally the straight-forward, no tricks (update: These days it’s probably: Fewer tricks) faction that wins games by shooting the opponent off the table and then worrying about the scoreboard later.

We’re going to discuss each of PanO’s sectorials first and then round up with the vanilla/generic army list at the end. So, without further ado…
Kestrel Colonial Force

We here at Goonhammer HQ workshopped nicknames for Kestrel Colonial Force and after 16 gruelling hours of arguments the vote was that Kestrel, the PanO sectorial based on a desert planet, would be known as SandO. However, I’m going to just straight up ignore that and lean into the common typo of KFC. So, anytime I’ve written ‘KFC’ just assume that I’m being incredibly funny on purpose [Ed: No, it’s not] and not making an oblique reference to a certain Colonel with 11 Secret Herbs and Spices. Or, maybe I am? Do you even know what Helot tastes like?
Play Style and Faction Identity
Kestrel Colonial Force (photo courtesy of Corvus Belli)

I don’t know how to describe Kestrel playstyle and identity except as ‘PanO Goodstuff’. You’ll struggle to wander through the army list without tripping over a good profile. If we had to drill down further, I’d note that Kestrel doesn’t have the absolute peak of firepower (ie. BS 15 HRMC TAGs) but it still has some absolutely solid firepower (and the Hetkari, which is an A+ shooter all by itself). Your infowar capability is probably mostly there to give you the breathing room you need to win the shooting war, it’s an enabler function rather than a core function of the faction.
Fireteams

Pretty standard layout here, 1 Fireteam Core, 1 Haris, unlimited Duos. Wildcards are fine, good balance of specialists, mid-tier shooters and toolbox units.
Fennec Fireteams: Core or Haris

Being an LI Fireteam, it’s no surprise that we can’t have a Duo here, which is a shame, as having a two Fennec combo of a HMG and Paramedic for the +1 dice shooting is incredibly efficient. Understandably, we can’t do this because unlimited Duos would be exploited and the game would become degenerate very quickly. As it stands, just plug in an additional Fennec and make a Haris or Core. Which is fine, nobody ever complained about paying 10 points for a BS 12 Combi-rifle that generates an order.
Kestrel Fireteams: Duo

This fireteam seems useful but potentially expensive if you’re trying to get a Burst bonus onto an Active piece. I feel like the trick would be to attach a Wildcard specialist to your choice of shooter: Griffin HMG or Hetkari and go wild (Bipandra might be an option, because she can also heal the active piece if they lose too many F2F rolls).
Armoured Fireteams: Duo

I’m pretty open-minded and do my best to avoid constricting my thinking to ‘What’s good in N4’ but I don’t think it’s a struggle to suggest that Double-TAG fireteams aren’t going to be terribly popular or efficient. Prove me wrong, children. Prove me wrong. Honestly, what you might see here is a TAG dragging a wildcard specialist around, most likely the Pathfinder which can keep up with it (while also delivering a Repeater into the mid-field). Maybe you’ll drop in a Bulleteer to give you a sacrificial attack piece? The choice is yours. It’s fine. Basically, if you’re going to take a Tikbalang or Squalos on their own merits as a solo piece, you may as well duct-tape a Pathfinder to it.
Colcorp Fireteams: Haris or Duo

FT Master on 6 point Jackals is one helluva drug. Haris or Duo, you’ve got cheap Regular orders that can pull double-duty as ARO pieces. If you have points and Combat Group slots left over I can think of worse things to do than fill them with ColCorp Duos. These seem very solid, and I’m leaning towards the Multi Marksman Rifle on the Magistrate personally.
Notable Units

Just to help you navigate your way through your first Kestrel Fried Chicken combo-meal, here’s our top pick for units to watch out for as you play your first games in N5:

Hetkari Shooters: Hoo boy. This heckin’ chonker is an Apex Predator. I think that while if you did the maths on how often BS 14 with Marksmanship and BS Attack -3 actually wins Firefights you might be mildly and pleasantly surprised, I’m still going to lean into the MULTI Red Fury because I am addicted to rolling as many dice as possible. At least with an AP-ammo type you can convert F2F wins into damage against harder targets, so the Multi-Fury (much easier to say than the full name) remains a very viable weapon. Regardless of loadout, you’re still spending about 1/6th of your total points for a single 6-2 S2 model that starts in your DZ and has no mobility skills. A poor deployment here could see you handicapped for the rest of your game, where larger models (ie. TAGs) or those with movement skills are more likely to get the LOF they need to recover from an unlucky deployment choice. So, Hetkari Shooter: They’re great, amazing even, when they start rolling F2F but it’s your job to make that happen.

Redeye Close Air Support Squad: Noting above that the Hektari might struggle to line up some good fights, the Redeye is a hyper-mobile platform that won’t struggle to start a fight in a bar on a Friday night. Whether it can win those fights is another question. As a side note, check out that Dodge -3 and consider the damage you might be able to do by Spec Firing that Grenade Launcher. Vehicles appear to sit in the design space between TAGs and HI: they’re mobile, have some resilience (ie. decent Arm and multiple STR), and good but not amazing F2F capabilities (BS 13 here, Mimetism -6…but also No Cover), you also have to pay elite HI/budget-TAG prices for them.

Fireflies: These are a very multi-role unit which binds some important KCF Fireteams together. They can be great support for TAGs when taken as engineers, they allow NCO Orders to be spent moving a team, and they provide the only turret Minelayer currently in the game. Really the envy of other factions’ Fireteam MI.

Magistrates (and Jackals): The former give PanO a really top-class command structure, with Counterintelligence and Strategos for an unbelievably cheap price. While natural Lts, we can also support them as Chain of Command for one of the Sectorial’s superb active Lt options, e.g. the Tikbalang with +1 Lt Order. Their other utility is making Jackals Regular Order generators via Fireteam Master, which is just an insane value.

Father Lucien Sforza: A late addition to the team, Sforza is an odd addition to the roster. He suffers from the curse of having enough skills and capability to be expensive but not enough capability to be a reliable threat to the sorts of pieces you might want him to be facing off against. If you really desperately need a 0 SWC weapon that can shoot out to HMG rangebands, I guess you can give Sforza a call. Alternatively, if you absolutely must have a bounty-hunting priest with a glue-gun in your list, he’s your man.

Rating: S

If KFC has any weaknesses as a faction I am yet to encounter them. Maybe infowar but it’s pretty hard to suggest that a faction with access to Peacemakers and Marker-state Hackers has weak Hacking.
Sample List

To help you write your first KFC lists, we’ve provided a delicious free sample! Disclaimer: Goonhammer advises that the free sample list contains none of the 11 secret herbs and spices, almost no actual chicken (or Kestrel) meat, and has no nutritional value whatsoever. Any victories achieved while using this list are not intended and entirely coincidental. If your LDL cholesterol levels stay above 160 mg/dl for longer than 3 hours after a game please see your Doctor.

Army list: Finger Lickin’ Good



This isn’t an optimised KFC list by any stretch of the imagination but you’ve got a whole heap of defence in depth to ensure that you survive your opponent’s alpha strike. A lot of these pieces can also turn around and prosecute an attack run as needed, so you’ll always have options to take a chunk out of your opponent even if they get to swing first.
Svalarheima Winter Force
Play Style and Faction Identity

Svalarheima is PanO with highly mobile Close Combat elements. It’s insane how many pieces you can get that have Super-Jump and a CC score above 20. It’s Three, which isn’t a lot but it’s weird it’s happening in PanO. Also, Valkyrie. Svalarheima are, generally, a very honest faction. You don’t get a huge amount of fog of war on deployment as your opponent will look at your list and have a pretty good idea of what you’re bringing to the game. That said, SWF did finally learn how to use camouflage: They finally have multiple units with a marker state and even a few Minelayers to help mix it up but your opponents probably won’t be surprised twice in any one game by your list.
Credit: Robert “TheChirurgeon” Jones
Fireteams
Fusilier Fireteam

Pretty simple and generic. Take some Fusiliers, maybe splice in a gun (Oktavia, Karhu or a Wildcard), call it done. Drop in a Hacker if you want the cheapest possible way to leverage the area denial of Peace Makers.
Nisses Fireteam

My default build here would be a Nisse HMG or Sniper backed up by a Machinist WinterFor. Get that sweet +1SD for your big gun, drag a specialist around with you. Seems solid. You’re a braver person than I if you go for a Nisse Haris or full Core.
WinterFor Alpha Fireteam

Building up a fireteam that needs to start with an ORC or Varg is going to be an expensive proposition. I personally woldn’t do much more than a pure Duo. I’d be very tempted to roll in Valkyrie as her unique melee capabilities and survivability makes her an excellent Trading-Up piece. This is also notable as the Super-Jump team, for Vargar with or without Shona Carano. Bouncing 6-6 around the table is a lot of fun.
WinterFor Beta Fireteam

Another potentially expensive Duo/Haris but at least you can either do Double-Hospitaller (Doc and HMG is the first order optimal pick) or ‘Any shooter’ tailed by an Infirmarer. Seems perfectly fine. The Boyg is very expensive but represents an excellent MSV-breaking piece (and SWF loves a cheeky bit of Mimetism, so having someone to chew on your natural predators feels like a solid play.), so having them tailed by a specialist (native member or wildcard) while they sweep the ramparts is a perfectly reasonable choice.
Bulleteers Duo

Does exactly what it says on the box. You can maybe strap a Karhu Engineer to the back of a Bulleteer but that feels like the Tail is Wagging the Dog. That said, at 19 points with the option to repair, a Bulleteer HSG is going to trade up if it can get into the 0-8” rangeband.
Notable Units

Let’s not beat around the bush…

Jotum: It’s an ARM 10, BTS 9 monstrosity. If it’s in cover, a combi-rifle cannot hurt it. Jotum is like Chuck Norris. It doesn’t sleep, it lies awake at night and waits. A Taigha Creature Berserked into the Jotum once and after three agonising rounds, the Taigha Creature died.

Vargar and Boyg: I’m putting both of these into the same category as they are both Albedo -6 units. SWF loves a bit of Mimetism, so these guys are great insurance against MSV ruining your day.

Liang Kai: Liang Kai is the warband most warbands wish they were. Playing PanO, you’ve got the long-range game sorted, so Liang Kai is the man you send in to mop up once your opponent has flinched and ducked into cover. To a lesser extent, Shona Carano and Valkyrie can do the same but start with Liang Kai and work from there.

Team Deus Vult: Despite being categorically not Military Orders, apparently SWF does get representation from the religious side of PanO. Hawkins is a great little piece that does everything, the Knight of Justice is great for the price (and the Hacker, at BTS 9 is amazing), while the regular Hospitallers are a little slow (foot slogging out of your DZ at 4-4), they’re well priced for their ability to win fights

Rating: B+

Svalarheima seems pretty good. They’ve got PanO guns, great close-quarters pieces, a sprinkling of Knights and the Jotum. What else could you want?
Sample List

Just to help you start off with writing Svalarheima lists, we’ve provided a sample list below. This list contains some elements of the faction that we think you’d do well to explore and experiment with at the start of the N5 edition cycle. Disclaimer: Goonhammer advises that the following list is for demonstration purposes only. Any victories achieved while using these lists are not intended and entirely coincidental. If your hypothermia persists for longer than 3 hours after a game please drink a nice warm cup of hot cocoa (and then see a doctor).


So much close quarters combat. If your Nisse sniper and Karhu Feurbach and get your opponent to duck, your Murder Squad will be able to leap across the table and do horrible, nasty things to them.
Generic PanO

We’ve decided to put the parent-faction, Generic PanO at the bottom of the list instead of first because we’re going to be talking about a few profiles that were already going to be covered in their respective Sectorials. So, rather than duplicate words or endlessly say ‘see below’, we’re just going to hope you’ve read the previous sections above and know what we’re on about. First of all, I just need to call out Corvus Belli for what they did to my boy, Varuna. In memory of my glorious beach-party faction I will henceforth be referring to vanilla PanO as Varunilla, because that’s where all the Varuna refugees ended up (Cutters, Crocmen, Zulu Cobras, etc etc). I will force this meme so hard.

Play Style and Faction Identity

It’s PanO, they’re schtick has been having high-water mark shooters for the longest time. They’ve still got that. They’ve got the Hektari, the Crux Knight, as well as the Cutter. However, they’ve been given a few more tricks in this edition and if you fail to adapt to your new toolbox you’re going to get outflanked, stabbed and hacked before you can get your shots off.
Notable Units

Blade-Ops: Fireteam Glue, Blade Ops are great at plugging a Tactical Aware specialist into any Duo led by a shooter you want to run in vanilla. Does your TAG want a pocket Engineer for repairs? Done. Even if your attack piece falls over your Blade remains a BS-13 operative with Mimetism and an unceasing appetite for violence.

Bolts: With Neo-Capitaline Army deprecated the noble Bolts have been orphaned and then folded into vanilla PanO. Which is great for vanilla PanO, mind you. Being BS 13 platforms with Warhorse and Marksmanship, they’re surprisingly well priced and that’s before you consider that a Bolt Hacker is BTS 6 and functionally immune to Oblivion. You need to have a good reason to drop a Bolt into your lists but they’ve got some cute tech going, so it’s rarely hard to justify the choice. Have at it.

Crux Knight: PanO doesn’t get amazing fireteam options in vanilla (Except for the Heloans, trust me, all will be explained) but who needs them when you have a knight that brings their own fireteam bonus with them? I’m just sayin’, Crux Knight bought the N5: Season Pass and they’re going to make it your problem. Great shooter, baked in Specialist, just enough melee to keep your opponents honest and then a cheeky dash of Albedo tech, just because. So good.

Cutter: For when you absolutely must shoot BS 15 bullets at people while inflicting a Mimetism -6 modifier on them before scuttling away and hiding under a Camo Marker. The Cutter remains a top tier TAG in both firepower and survivability. Only available in Vanilla PanO. Think about it.

Swiss Guard: For when you want to pay half the price of a Cutter for slightly more than half the performance of a Cutter. Or a 2W Noctifer, whatever floats your boat. I don’t rate them terribly highly based on their deployment options and speed but you’re still getting Apex Predator levels of firepower for a reasonable price. Worth noting as a capability unique to vanilla PanO but not an auto-take either.

Joan of Arc: The Joan and the Irregulars list is still an option in N5, even if you can’t lean hard into it (which makes for a better list, honestly.). She’s still a 2.5W CC monster that shoots quite. Also, she’s got a new AP-Spitfire. Brilliant.

Fireteams

So, Heloans [portmanteau: Helot plus Joan]. We had to come to this eventually. Joan and two Helots. It’s a thing. It’s a real, danged good thing. You could do the same thing with Patsy but if you take Joan you’ve just turned those Helots Regular and life gets good when you have Regular Helots in Fireteams.

    Genghis Cohen: while Joan and Helots is very impressive, I think deploying Regular Helots in the best possible defensive positions is at least as good. What I was most struck by is the potential of teaming Joan up with 2 Blade-Ops for an active team with 3 Tactical Orders.

PanO also retains some terrific Duo options for TAGs, not pure Duos so much as utility models and engineers supporting their Remote Presence war machines – although you can make a pure Squalo Duo if you feel like it! It would also be remiss not to mention the efficiency of Karhu or Bolt pure Duos. Those are teams that deliver an apex shooter and a very handy specialist in one neat package.

Rating: A

Vanilla PanO brings top-tier shooting in a game that features a disturbing amount of shooting and, with their tweaks coming into N5, a wide and deep enough toolbox that the solution to every problem doesn’t have to be Gun or More Gun (Apologies and thanks to the humble readers that noticed the missing rating for Varunilla, we thank you for your service).

Yes, technically it’s only got 14 troopers but between the Tactical Awareness Orders and plethora of Specialists and Attack Pieces, this list is a blast to play. If you really need those 15 troopers, cut the ORC in half, King Solomon-styles.
Conclusion

Well, that’s PanO and its three sectorials for you. CB has made the right move here and expanded PanO’s toolbox, giving veterans more options and playstyles to engage with while ensuring that newer PanO players still get a taste of overwhelming firepower, just as God intended. We’ll release individual faction reviews for Varunilla and the three sectorials in the future but, for the time being, we hope this is enough to get your engine started. Get out there and do a Hyper-Capitalism to your foes, if you can’t buy their loyalty with your superior PanO economy, blow them into submission with your superior PanO firepower.
--- End of PanOceania Impressions.txt ---

which may not necessarily stack all the bonuses together but form a very useful

Playstyle

game. Tactical flexibility and utility are central to getting the most from them. This is a sector rule for players
who like to get up close then unleash hell. With a wealth of close combat and shorter range troopers, the midfield is
a good place for this force to play. And we'll cover that a little bit in a minute. Actually, when you look at things like the tags, you do have a tag
with an HMG, but you also have a tag with a multi- Spitfire. Now, a multi- Spitfire is unpleasant in many, many

more things that fit with what that tag is designed to do. The HMG is kind of
almost disadvantageous to that particular chassis. So, the midfield is kind of where you want to be positioning
yourself and getting in the enemy's face. You can do long-range engagements. There are some units inside of this
force which are phenomenal at long range, but for the most part, you're getting in the enemy's face and you're making them regret the fact they got up
this morning. That is the Shindom Mutai way. That said, they're not without capable long-range fire support units
and solo hit pieces. There are some beautiful solo hit pieces in here. Some of which offers unique features and

Lore
The history of the greater independent Japan is a long and painful one. The
story of the modern nation begins when the Japanese nation was annexed by Yuching. Though done via treaty, the
implications of refusal were clear. Sign or suffer.
But still, for generations, the Japanese then suffered. At first, they were accepted, but as their desire to keep
their own identity was noted, then regarded as suspicious, they were forced to live as secondclass citizens. So a
need to retain tradition turned to resentment then rebellion culminating in the uprising.
The events leading up to the uprising are many and varied but the final straw was undoubtedly the incident at the
Baying Orbital Consulate. In essence, a retaliatory strike on a Nomad torpedo
ship docked at the station was ordered. Even though the Yuching command knew it
would kill many Yuching troopers. But as these troopers were Japanese, it wasn't
viewed as an issue and the strike was carried out. When news of this broke, the Japanese
citizens had had enough. The fact that the high command was willing to sacrifice their troopers as if they were
nothing, killing their people, simply could not stand. Soon, mass
protests broke out in the streets across Yuching, focused around the centers of power and government. But even as the
Imperial Service crushed the rebellion, the damage was already done. A secret plan long in the making was enacted.
The Japanese would be free. With support from Panosania and the
nomad nations, the Japanese rose and rose again. Though the retribution of
the state nation forces was massive and devastating, the Japanese did not relent. Knowing
that they would need to take a legal route, the Japanese leadership worked hard and fast seeking official
recognition which would legally establish them as a state. When recognition was finally achieved,
the toll was heavy. But the Japanese were free with their own nation.
Though it wasn't a total victory, some Japanese states were not given independence and remain under the boot
of Yuching to this day. However, now free, the nation of greater
independent Japan is establishing its place in the human sphere. As a culture
rooted in Japanese tradition using the pillars of the emperor and the machido or the way of the samurai, the warrior
code is a cornerstone of this new society. Around these ancient ideas, the
Japanese secessionist army or JSA was established. A twist of fate would give greater
independent Japan luck. Whilst on a training mission, the attack ship
Thunderchild, a training ship managed by Panosania but crewed by JSA recruits,
received a message. The Thunderchild's Panosian captain, was told to cross the Abraasis wormhole and head towards the
newly discovered planet. Told to make all haste, he had no time to set the JSA
crew down anywhere safe. So once on the planet and with orders from JSA high command, the Japanese crew
mutinied, taking the ship for themselves and claiming the planet for greater independent Japan.
Needless to say, Panosani were none too happy with this betrayal. But still, the Japanese pressed ahead, colonizing the
planet Shinju as their own, whilst other nations came, including Panosania.
Unfortunately, the JSA were unable to conquer the whole planet, leaving them vulnerable as these interlopers
established their own colonies on the planet. This coupled with the discovery of ruins
and technology from an ancient race on Shinju led to the creation of the Shinden Bhai, an elite force which
combines the ancient samurai traditions with futuristic cutting technology,
enforcing the emperor's will across Shinju and keeping their enemies under
close watch.

--- Start of Some strategies in JSA.txt ---
JSA strategy question to avoid the kamikaze charge

Game Strategies for the JSA Faction and its Sub-Factions Shidenbutai and Oban in Infinity the Game
https://www.reddit.com/r/InfinityTheGame/comments/1ii5nt4/jsa_strategy_question/


#### Introduction

The JSA (Japanese Sectorial Army) faction in Corvus Belli's Infinity game is known for its versatility and unique abilities, particularly in close combat (CC). However, it's crucial not to rely solely on CC as your first line of attack, as CC is just one tool among many. Here's a detailed guide to playing strategies for the JSA and its subfactions Shidenbutai and Oban.


#### Using ranged attacks (BS)

- BS Attack Effectiveness: Every army, including the JSA, must use ranged attacks (BS) effectively. This is often the surest way to solve problems on the battlefield. The JSA has several solid units for shooting, such as Karakuri, Ruishi MSV2, and heavy infantry units (Hatamoto, Domaru, Daiyokai). Prioritize ARO pieces that can block your assault and opposing strategic pieces.

- Multispectral visors: Bring along one or two multispectral visors to improve the targeting accuracy of your shots and obtain additional tactical possibilities such as being able to shoot through smoke where the enemy can't, especially against units using mimetism.


#### Movement and Cover

- Prudent Movement: Use the Prudent Movement rule coupled with Stealth to move from one cover to another without risking being killed. This minimizes enemy AROs (Automatic Reaction Orders).

- Smoke, Discoball and Eclipse: Units such as Shikamis and those equipped with Discoball can use smoke and eclipse to move unseen. This makes it possible to get close to the enemy, to move towards a shelter or an advantageous firing position, or to cut off opposing lines of fire to move other troops forward.
You'll need to plan how you're going to place these occulting elements, and therefore how many orders you'll need to place them.
:point_right: Tip article: Infinity Tactics: Start Smoking https://www.goonhammer.com/infinity-tactics-start-smoking/

- Camouflage and Hidden Movement: Hidden deployment and camouflage can be a good way to get close to an opponent. Remember that most units have camouflage + stealth, so your opponent can't make an ARO if he doesn't have line of sight on you. Try to get behind him, or close the distance with smoke/eclipse/discoball, then force him into close combat that way.


#### Specific units and tactics

- Yojimbo: Use Yojimbo to throw smoke for free with his impetuous order. To do this effectively, plan to place him intelligently so that he can place them in a row. These smoke can cover the movements of other units as well as himself.

- Samurai fireteams: in Vanilla or Oban, Tanko Missile Launcher fireteams and a Domaru Spitfire are effective in eliminating AROs from a distance, thanks to their bonus firepower, while retaining good CC capacity.
Another similar fireteam idea, the Tanko option discoballer has been very successful with the Mushashi duo, Musashi can also go into a Domaru duo or haris. Having 2-3 samurai duos running around the table supported by discoballs and Yojimbo's smoke is quite fun.

- Ryuken: Ryuken units offer excellent potential for slowing down opponents with mimetism -6 and lethal support in hacking or guided missiles.

- Hatamotos, Kyojin and Tanukis: In Shindenbutai, Hatamotos are excellent snipers, while Tanukis with their hacking and Pitchers, and Kyojin with their flamethrowers add an extra dimension to your strategy.

- Karakuri: this is a fairly versatile BS attack piece, rather solid and annoying, especially with an engineer nearby to relieve it.

- Rui shi msv2: For when your enemy uses mimetism. You can also use an Evo hacker to give it precision, making it really hostile on the active turn. Remotes are generally pretty cheap at JSA.

- Access to CrazyKoala: These autonomous weapons are very effective and are available in many places, as are the inexpensive Keisotsu and TAG O-Yoroi.

- Shikami: The Shikami has become an absolute must-have not only for its Eclipses, but also for its offensive capacity and its ability to move, enhanced by numerous skills such as Super Jump, Climbing Plus and Stealth.

- Keisotsu: The new Keisotsu are really good. A Core fireteam consisting solely of a hacker, a thunderbolt and a Kempeitai Chain of Command can successfully eliminate a troublesome piece, take an objective, make a classified objective and launch crazy koala even if the rest of the assault list has fallen. They're not incredible, but they're surprisingly competent for their price range.


#### Close Combat Strategies (CC)

- Fear Projection: JSA's CC capability is more of a deterrent threat than a primary assault strategy. It is used to punish enemy troops who get too close, which can make opponents fearful of staying too close to charge.

- Using Stealth: Use the Stealth skill coupled with smoke to move without causing ARO and eliminate enemy pieces, especially if you're not visible at the start and end of your move, this allows you to move even in front of the enemy (as long as you're outside the Zone of Control).

- Assassinate ARO Pieces: Use ninjas to assassinate or immobilize troublesome ARO pieces, then shoot the rest. Once you've controlled the middle of the table, you can start using CC. Opposing weapons are generally at their weakest during an ARO ( for example, a combi rifle is 3 bursts on an active turn, while an ARO reaction is 1 burst).


#### Shidenbutai sub-faction

- Hatamotos, Kyojin and Tanukis: In Shindenbutai, Hatamotos are excellent snipers, while Tanukis hackers and Kyojin with their flamethrowers add an extra dimension to your strategy.


#### Sub-Faction Oban

- Focused on assault: Oban is highly concentrated in ranges under 24 inches, and often struggles to lead ranged battles outside this zone. Unfortunately, her mid-table deployment options are pretty crappy (Ryuken which can't be deployed as camouflage markers, and hidden but expensive deployment ninjas and Oniwaban).

- Saito Togan and Kitsune: Saito Togan is a cheaper option for infiltrating with smoke just outside enemy deployment, while Kitsune is better for killing units in an alpha strike through smoke.

- Heavy Infantry Fireteam: A 5-man fireteam with at least one Tanko Missile Launcher and a Domaru Spitfire can eliminate key ARO pieces at range and pose a threat at medium and short range, and for eliminating ARO pieces. If you don't make Core, you can also use Haris. In addition, HIs are quite fast with their 6-2 MOV.

- Daiyokai HMG: The Daiyokai with HMG option is useful for a slightly longer range than the other profiles.

- Shikami and Karakuri: These units are very versatile and have been upgraded to be more useful.

- O-Yoroi Kidobutai: A solid TAG that gives you a really good long-range weapon to serve as a Long-Range ORA.


#### Global strategies

- Infiltration and Alpha Strike: Use a camouflaged killer like Shinobu Kitsune or an Oniwaban to hit the enemy's biggest unit on turn 1 (such as the opposing HI Alpha strike and/or the Lieutenant). This forces the enemy to mined the corridors to their TAG or HI, thus wasting time and precious orders defending themselves.

- Objective capture: Use smoke or eclipses to cover objectives and take them with specialist ninja troops. It's pretty silly, but you'll trade any effective firefight for an objective capture.

- Basic maneuvers: Master basic unit maneuvers and the smoke/eclipse/discoball screen to put yourself in an awkward position for the opponent and allow you to attack effectively. You can also pose corner threats to force opponents to make a choice between passing through and risking an attack or going elsewhere.
You can also use the speed of motorcycles, which you can link in Duo to move quickly around the table (like the KHD version for assassinating opposing hackers or taking objectives, coupled with the Spitfire profile).
Or the range offered by Super-Jump and similar skills.
This extended range of movement can help avoid AROs who can't be everywhere.

- Hacking vulnerability: Be aware of hacking vulnerability in the reactive turn phase. Use Ninja KHDs to capture objectives and put a Reset on enemy hackers.


#### Conclusion

The JSA is a subtle faction that requires a good understanding of movement and cover tactics. By making effective use of smoke, stealth units and fireteams, you can control the battlefield and punish enemy mistakes. The Shidenbutai and Oban sub-factions offer unique options that can be exploited to maximize your gameplay potential. 
--- End of Some strategies in JSA.txt ---
